<a id="pm-s1"></a>
## 1. Abstract and result contract

> We prove the saturation conjecture for $`\mathop{\mathrm{Spin}}\nolimits (2n)`$, <i>n</i> ≥ 2. If three dominant integral weights sum to an element of the root lattice, then the existence of a nonzero tensor invariant after a positive integral dilation implies the existence of one at the original weights.

- **claim-type-d-saturation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-main)): Let $G=\mathop{\mathrm{Spin}}(2n)$, $n\ge2$, and let $\lambda,\mu,\nu$ be dominant integral weights such that $\lambda+\mu+\nu\in Q(D_n)$. Then, for every positive integer $N$, $$\bigl(V(\lambda)\otimes V(\mu)\otimes V(\nu)\bigr)^G\ne0 \quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad \bigl(V(N\lambda)\otimes V(N\mu)\otimes V(N\nu)\bigr)^G\ne0.$$
  - Explanation: Under the root-lattice congruence, a nonzero tensor invariant at any positive integral dilation of three dominant weights of the spin group forces one at the original weights. The converse is the easy direction.
- **claim-basic-real-schedule** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-basic-schedule)): Let the root system be of type $D$ or a permutation system, possibly with an orthogonal central space. If $(\lambda,\mu,\xi)$ has a real moment solution, then every shape $\eta$ ending at $\mu$ admits a schedule whose switch roots form a real basis of the semisimple space. Weak signs, endpoint times, and coincident times are allowed. More precisely, it is a limit of schedules for nearby regular data whose switches are covers in their local chamber frames. Simplifying the limiting schedule may leave fewer than rank-many switches.
  - Explanation: A real moment solution yields, for every shape, a schedule whose switch roots form a real basis, obtained as a limit of schedules for regular data whose switches are Bruhat covers.
- **claim-highest-coefficient** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-coefficient)): Let $h,h',\theta$ be dominant integral weights. The linear forms obtained by taking the highest-output coordinate of $$T(v_h\otimes {\cdot}),\qquad T:V(h)\otimes V(\theta)\longrightarrow V(h'),$$ are exactly the forms supported at weight $h'-h$ that annihilate $$\label{real:coefficient-images} \sum_i f_i^{h\cdot\alpha_i+1}V(\theta).$$ In particular, if $h'=h+u\theta$ is dominant, the extremal-coordinate functional at $u\theta$ gives such an intertwiner and is nonzero on the flag vector at $u$.
  - Explanation: Highest-to-highest coordinates of intertwiners are exactly the forms at the difference weight that kill the stated lowering images. An extremal functional gives a nonzero one.
- **claim-flag-composition** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-flag-composition)): Suppose that $h_0,\ldots,h_s$ and $\theta_1,\ldots,\theta_s$ are dominant integral weights and that there are intertwiners $$T_j:V(h_{j-1})\otimes V(\theta_j)\longrightarrow V(h_j).$$ Assume their highest-to-highest coefficients are nonzero on flag vectors at flags $b_j$. If the past and future limits of $b_j$ under $a(t)$ are $u_j,v_j$, and $v_j\ge u_{j+1}$, then the iterated composition is nonzero on $$V(h_0)\otimes V(\theta_1+\cdots+\theta_s).$$ Here the second factor is the Cartan summand in the tensor product of the $V(\theta_j)$.
  - Explanation: Intertwiners with nonzero highest-to-highest coefficients at flags ordered by Bruhat comparison compose to a map that is nonzero on the Cartan summand.
- **claim-invariants-give-moments** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-invariant-moment)): A nonzero invariant in $V(\lambda)\otimes V(\mu)\otimes V(\xi^*)$ gives a real moment solution for $(\lambda,\mu,\xi)$. The same conclusion follows from an invariant for simultaneous positive integer multiples of the labels.
  - Explanation: A nonzero invariant, possibly at simultaneous integer multiples of the labels, produces a real moment solution by maximizing a norm of the invariant evaluation.
- **claim-schedules-give-moments** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-schedule-moment)): A real schedule for $(\lambda,\eta,\xi)$ gives a real moment solution for $(\lambda,\mu,\xi)$, where $\mu=\eta(1)$. No lattice condition is required. The statement also holds for root subsystems with arbitrary orthogonal central coordinates.
  - Explanation: Any real schedule gives a real moment solution, with no lattice condition, by perturbing, composing extremal intertwiners and passing to a limit.
- **claim-strict-approximation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-real-strict-approximation)): Every real moment triple can be approximated by triples of regular labels admitting strict flags. Such strict label triples form an open set. In particular, after choosing a nearby regular shape, there is open freedom to vary its two endpoint labels independently.
  - Explanation: Real moment triples are limits of regular label triples with strict flags, and these strict triples form an open set, giving freedom to vary endpoints.
- **claim-symmetric-space-triangles** (theorem, [source](#stmt-real-triangles)): If $(\lambda,\mu,\xi^*)$ admits strict flags, then for every $R>0$ there is a triangle in $\mathcal G/K$ with successive dominant side vectors $R\lambda,R\mu,R\xi^*$.
  - Explanation: Strict flags give triangles of every scale in the symmetric space with the prescribed dominant side vectors, using a fixed point of composed pushes.
- **claim-decreasing-radial-paths** (theorem, [source](#stmt-real-decreasing-paths)): For generic strict labels and a generic regular polygonal shape ending at $\mu$, there is a dominant path from $\lambda$ to $\xi$ whose velocities are Weyl translates of the shape velocities, in weakly Bruhat-decreasing order.
  - Explanation: Rescaled triangle sides converge to a dominant path whose velocities are Weyl translates of the shape velocities in weakly Bruhat-decreasing order.
- **claim-area-variation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-real-variation)): For an area-maximizing path with regular shape, the velocity differences at its nonzero unfolded jumps away from shape breaks are linearly independent. Here all differences are measured in the common unfolded coordinate space.
  - Explanation: At an area maximum with regular shape the velocity differences at nonzero unfolded jumps away from shape breaks are linearly independent, since a dependency would give an improving variation.
- **claim-strict-exposure** (theorem, [source](#stmt-real-exposure)): For such a generic area maximum, let $u\to v$ be a nonzero jump in local chamber coordinates.* 1. *If the site is not a shape break and the slope is $d$, there is a linear functional $f$ whose maximum among $\{hd:v\le h\le u\}$ is attained exactly at $ud$ and $vd$.* 2. *If it is a shape break with slopes $d_-,d_+$, a common functional $f$ has strict maximum at $ud_-$ among the corresponding $d_-$-vectors, and strict maximum at $vd_+$ among the corresponding $d_+$-vectors.
  - Explanation: At a generic area maximum each nonzero jump is exposed by a linear functional that singles out its endpoint states, strictly at shape breaks.
- **claim-flag-orbit-polytopes** (theorem, [source](#stmt-real-polytope)): Choose a flag $b\in U^+u\cap U^-v$, with $u\ge v$. The torus-fixed points of its torus-orbit closure lie in $[v,u]$ and include $u,v$. For a regular dominant real weight $d$, take the convex hull of the corresponding vectors $hd$. Its normal fan, with vertices labeled by fixed flags, is independent of $d$. Every edge joins two vertices related by a root reflection.
  - Explanation: The torus-orbit closure of a flag in a Schubert intersection has a weight polytope with a fan independent of the regular weight, and every edge is a root reflection.
- **claim-exposed-jumps-covers** (theorem, [source](#stmt-real-covers)): The generic area maximum has no nonzero jump at a shape break. Every other nonzero unfolded jump is a reflection and is a Bruhat cover in its local chamber frame.
  - Explanation: The generic area maximum has no jump at shape breaks, and every other nonzero jump is a reflection that is a Bruhat cover.
- **claim-no-third-axis-obstruction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-real-blocker)): At a switch of the limiting schedule in Proposition <a href="#prop:basic-schedule" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="prop:basic-schedule">3</a>, suppose the two exchanged signed labels have strict opposite position and moving-value orders. No signed label on a third physical axis can lie strictly between them in both orders. The moving values are read immediately before the switch, including its specified position in a simultaneous cluster.
  - Explanation: The cover property forbids a third-axis label strictly between two exchanged labels in both orders at a switch.
- **claim-integral-schedules** (theorem, [source](#stmt-thm-integral-schedule)): Let the three labels have integral root pairings and satisfy $\lambda+\mu-\xi\in Q$, and let $\eta$ be any polygonal shape with dominant increments and endpoint $\mu$. A real moment solution gives an integral schedule in either of the following cases:* 1. *the root system is a permutation system;* 2. *the root system is $D_l$, and $\mu$ has at most one zero coordinate.* *An orthogonal central space is allowed in either case; its equation is ordinary vector addition.
  - Explanation: Under integral root pairings and the lattice condition, a real moment solution gives an integral schedule for permutation systems and for type D when the moving weight has at most one zero coordinate.
- **claim-block-reduction** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-block-reduction)): Suppose a moment solution splits into commuting coordinate blocks of permutation type and orthogonal type. Assign the initial, moving, and final coordinates using this splitting. Under the hypotheses of Theorem <a href="#thm:integral-schedule" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:integral-schedule">16</a><span class="upright">(ii)</span>, integral schedules can be combined from the blocks. In particular, induction applies if there is a permutation block, if there are at least three proper orthogonal blocks, or if there are two proper orthogonal blocks of even parity. Here the parity of an orthogonal block is the sum, modulo two, of its assigned difference $\lambda+\mu-\xi$.
  - Explanation: Integral schedules can be assembled from commuting permutation and orthogonal blocks, so induction applies in the stated block configurations.
- **claim-exceptional-case** (classification, [source](#stmt-lem-hard-case)): If no integral schedule exists, any real schedule with at most $l$ switches has exactly $l$ independent switch roots, and its graph has exactly two connected components, both unbalanced and of odd parity. Each component is unicyclic. On its cycle the switch coefficients are half-odd-integers, and off the cycle they are integers.
  - Explanation: Failure of integral realizability forces a rigid configuration: exactly two unbalanced unicyclic odd components with half-odd-integral coefficients on the cycles and integers elsewhere.
- **claim-closedness-generic-shapes** (theorem, [source](#stmt-int-closedness)): For fixed labels, the existence of an integral schedule is closed under uniform convergence of shapes with dominant increments. It suffices to prove Theorem <a href="#thm:integral-schedule" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:integral-schedule">16</a> for polygonal shapes whose slopes are relatively interior to the smallest chamber face containing $\mu$, with generic break values in that face.
  - Explanation: Existence of integral schedules is closed under uniform convergence of shapes, so generic shapes in the face of the moving weight suffice.
- **claim-half-kernel** (theorem, [source](#stmt-int-half-kernel)): Suppose the $l+1$ roots generate $Q(D_l)$, and their exact base coefficients $m^0$ are half-integral. If $m^0$ is not already integral, either orientation $d$ of half the primitive integer relation among the roots satisfies $$\label{int:half-bounds} m^0+d\in\mathbb Z^{l+1},\qquad \|d_i\|\le1,\qquad \|d_i^G\|\le1,$$ where $d^G$ is its image under the linear part of <a href="#int:gap" data-reference-type="eqref" data-reference="int:gap">[int:gap]</a>. Moreover, there is a vector $L$ such that $$\label{int:potential-direction} d_i=L\cdot\gamma_i\quad(1\le i\le l+1).$$
  - Explanation: Half of the primitive relation among lattice-generating roots moves half-integral coefficients to integers with bounded entries, and it arises from a potential vector.
- **claim-continuation** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-continuation)): Under the standing assumption of no integral schedule, a local link cannot exist.
  - Explanation: A local link would be continued along the half-relation motion to parameter one, producing an integral schedule, so none exists under the standing assumption.
- **claim-direct-links** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-direct-links)): In the exceptional schedule with components $P,Q$, each of the following produces a forbidden local link:* 1. *a signed cross-component pair with strict concordant position and moving-value orders at time zero;* 2. *a signed cross-component pair with strict discordant orders at time one;* 3. *a position crossing of a cross-component pair with distinct moving values between old switch times;* 4. *the crossing in <span class="upright">(iii)</span> at an old switch time, if both axes are unused by its cluster.
  - Explanation: Four concrete cross-component configurations each construct a local link, hence are forbidden.
- **claim-profile-rigidity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-knot-profile-rigidity)): Suppose two irreducible Hermitian zero-sum triples have proportional profiles $m,m'$, of sizes $r,h$, respectively, and $$\\|m\\|^2=r^2,\qquad \\|m'\\|^2=h^2.$$ Then $r=h$ and $m=m'$.
  - Explanation: Irreducible Hermitian zero-sum triples with proportional profiles of saturated norm have equal size and equal profile, via flag varieties and Krull-Schmidt uniqueness.
- **claim-strict-count** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-strict-count)): In the standing exceptional configuration, $$\label{knot:strict-count} C:=R-p_\lambda-p_\mu-p_\xi>0.$$
  - Explanation: In the exceptional configuration the weighted number of cross comparisons strictly exceeds the total of cross ties at the initial, moving and final data.
- **claim-plateaus** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-plateau)): Suppose cross labels $a,c$ have equal position and equal assigned moving value on an open interval between switch times. Then $$\label{knot:plateau-phases} (p_a,p_c)=(0,0)\quad\hbox{or}\quad(1,1).$$
  - Explanation: On a double-tie interval the core phases of the two labels are both at the start value or both at the end value.
- **claim-knot-monotonicity** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-knot-count)): Under the standing no-integral-schedule assumption, the function $J$ is nondecreasing at every knot, and hence on the whole interval in the one-sided sense used above.
  - Explanation: The comparison function never decreases at a knot, since any loss yields a forbidden link or is offset by a plateau contribution.
- **claim-knot-matching** (theorem, [source](#stmt-knot-matching)): Within each two-value class at a knot, the transfers of the high value form disjoint matching pairs inside each component, together with unused single labels. In an opposite class the matching is disjoint on physical axes, with its mirror transfers understood as the same physical switches.
  - Explanation: Transfers of the high value at a knot reduce to disjoint pairs and unused single labels, since otherwise fewer switches would suffice.
- **claim-column-intertwiners** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-columns)): Let $\lambda,\mu,\xi$ be dominant integral weights of type $D_n$, $n\ge2$. Choose a shape obtained by concatenating straight segments whose increments are fundamental weights and whose sum is $\mu$. If this shape admits an integral schedule from $\lambda$ to $W\xi$, then $$\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}_G\bigl(V(\lambda)\otimes V(\mu),V(\xi)\bigr)\ne0.$$ An empty list of columns is allowed when $\mu=0$.
  - Explanation: An integral schedule along a concatenation of fundamental columns gives a nonzero intertwiner from the tensor product onto the target representation.
- **claim-schedule-folding** (theorem, [source](#stmt-col-folding)): An integral schedule can be folded into a dominant path with Weyl direction states in decreasing Bruhat order. Its changes of state can be expressed as an ordered sequence of downward reflections, including at coincident times. At each such step, from $w$ to $s_\alpha w$ at time $t$, $$\label{col:folded-integrality} \alpha\cdot w\eta(t)\in\mathbb Z.$$ For a concatenation of fundamental columns, all column endpoint positions of the folded path are dominant integral weights.
  - Explanation: An integral schedule folds into a dominant path with Bruhat-decreasing states and integral pairings at each downward step.
- **claim-common-walls** (theorem, [source](#stmt-col-common-walls)): Let $J$ be the set of simple roots $\alpha_i$ with $x_0\cdot\alpha_i=x_1\cdot\alpha_i=0$. A functional supported on the weight space of weight $\delta$ in $\bigwedge^k E$, invariant under the semisimple group generated by $J$, is an allowed highest-to-highest coefficient for an intertwiner $V(x_0)\otimes V(\omega)\to V(x_1)$.
  - Explanation: A functional at the difference weight invariant under the common-wall subgroup is an allowed highest-to-highest coefficient for a column.
- **claim-zero-tail-removal** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-zero-tail)): Suppose $\lambda,\mu,\xi$ are dominant integral $D_n$-weights, all supported in coordinates $1,\ldots,r$, where $1\le r\le n-2$. If $V(\xi)$ occurs in $V(\lambda)\otimes V(\mu)$, then $$V(2\widehat\xi)\ \text{occurs in}\ V(2\widehat\lambda)\otimes V(2\widehat\mu) \quad\text{for }D_{r+1},$$ where $\widehat\lambda=(L_1,\ldots,L_r,0)$, and similarly for the other two labels.
  - Explanation: Occurrence for labels supported on an initial segment gives doubled occurrence for the truncated labels in a smaller even spin group.
- **claim-tensor-criterion** (theorem, [source](#stmt-prop-preprojective)): Let $\lambda,\mu,\xi$ be dominant integral weights for $\mathcal D$, and suppose $\lambda+\mu-\xi=\sum_i d_i\alpha_i$ with $d_i\in\mathbb Z_{\ge0}$. Put $\lambda_i=\lambda\cdot\alpha_i$ and $\mu_i=\mu\cdot\alpha_i$. Then $$\label{tail:tensor-criterion} \begin{aligned} &\mathop{\mathrm{Hom}}\bigl(V(\lambda)\otimes V(\mu),V(\xi)\bigr)\ne0\\ &\quad\Longleftrightarrow\quad \exists M\in\Lambda(d): \quad \operatorname{top}_i M\le\lambda_i,\quad \operatorname{soc}_i M\le\mu_i\quad\text{for every }i . \end{aligned}$$
  - Explanation: Tensor occurrence is equivalent to a preprojective module with top and socle dimensions bounded by the weight pairings.
- **claim-threshold-bounds** (bound, [source](#stmt-tail-threshold-bounds)): For every $1\le r\le p$ and $b\ge1$, $$\label{tail:threshold-result} K_r^T(b)\le L_r+\frac{q_r(b)}2,\qquad K_r^S(b)\le M_r+\frac{q_r(b)}2 .$$
  - Explanation: Threshold counts of rows at every filtration level are bounded by the label coordinate plus half the number of long rows, for tops and socles.
- **claim-filtered-intersection** (theorem, [source](#stmt-lem-filtered-intersection)): Let $$H=F_1\supseteq F_2\supseteq\cdots\supseteq F_{s+1}=0$$ be a filtration of a finite-dimensional complex vector space, and let $P\subseteq\mathop{\mathrm{GL}}(H)$ be its stabilizer. For subspaces $A,C$ write $a_b=\dim(A\cap F_b)$, $c_b=\dim(C\cap F_b)$, and $f_b=\dim F_b$. For $g$ in a nonempty Zariski-open subset of $P$, $$\label{tail:intersection-formula} \dim(A\cap gC)= \max\left(0,\max_{1\le b\le s}(a_b+c_b-f_b)\right).$$
  - Explanation: For generic elements of the stabilizer of a filtration the intersection dimension equals the largest level deficiency, shown by a nested-support matching argument.
- **claim-centralizer-socle** (theorem, [source](#stmt-tail-centralizer)): Let $t$ be nilpotent on a complex vector space $V$, and put $$F_b(t)=\ker t\cap\mathop{\mathrm{im}}t^{b-1}\qquad(b\ge1).$$ The group $$C(t)=\{g\in\mathop{\mathrm{GL}}(V):gt=tg\}$$ induces the full stabilizer of this filtration on $\ker t$. It also induces the full stabilizer of the corresponding filtration on $\ker t^*\subseteq V^*$, under the contragredient action. The algebraic group $C(t)$ is irreducible.
  - Explanation: The centralizer of a nilpotent operator induces the full stabilizer of the socle filtration, on the space and its dual, and is irreducible.
- **claim-fractional-stretching** (counterexample, [source](#stmt-tail-fractional)): Positive integral stretching is essential to the formulation. If arbitrary positive rational $N$ are allowed whenever all the scaled weights exist, the unscaled root-lattice condition alone does not suffice. For $D_n$, $n\ge4$, take $\lambda=\mu=\nu=2e_1$. The triangle contraction $$(x,y)(y,z)(z,x)$$ of the standard orthogonal form defines a nonzero invariant on $V(2e_1)^{\otimes3}$. Indeed isotropic squares lie in the highest-weight module $V(2e_1)$, and, in a hyperbolic basis $a_i,b_i$ with $(a_i,b_j)=\delta_{ij}$, the isotropic vectors $$a_1,\qquad b_1,\qquad a_1+b_1+a_2-b_2$$ have nonzero pairwise products. At $N=1/2$ the three labels are $e_1$, whose sum $3e_1$ is outside $Q(D_n)$; hence the triple has no invariant. This does not affect Theorem <a href="#thm:main" data-reference-type="ref" data-reference="thm:main">1</a>, where $N$ is a positive integer.
  - Explanation: Allowing rational stretching breaks the statement: a triple with an invariant has a rational dilation outside the root lattice, so integral stretching is essential.

        "exact_quote": "Suppose a moment solution splits into commuting coordinate blocks of permutation type and orthogonal type. Under the hypotheses of Theorem <a href=\"#thm:integral-schedule\" data-reference-type=\"ref\" data-reference=\"thm:integral-schedule\">16</a><span class=\"upright\">(ii)</span>, integral schedules can be combined from the blocks. <a id="pm-s2"></a>
## 2. Definitions, validity and bounds

- **schedule** ([source](#stmt-def-schedule)): A schedule for $(\lambda,\eta,\xi)$ starts at $\lambda$, ends at a point of $W\xi$, and, between finitely many ordered switch times, satisfies $y'=w\eta'$ for a fixed $w\in W$.
- **shape** ([source](#pm-h-4)): A *shape* ending at $\mu$ is a continuous polygonal path $\eta:[0,1]\longrightarrow\mathbb R^l$ with $\eta(0)=0$, $\eta(1)=\mu$, and every increment in $\mathcal C$.
- **regular shape** ([source](#pm-h-4)): It is *regular* if all its slopes lie in the open chamber.
- **real moment solution** ([source](#pm-h-4)): A *real moment solution* for $(\lambda,\mu,\xi)$ is a zero-sum triple in the compact orbits of $\lambda,\mu,-\xi$.
- **fundamental column** ([source](#sec:introduction)): A *fundamental column* is a straight path segment whose increment is a fundamental weight.
- **core phase** ([source](#pm-h-19)): For a signed position label $a$, define its *core phase* $p_a(t)$ on the open intervals between switch times.
- **preprojective representation** ([source](#pm-h-30)): A representation of the resulting doubled quiver is *preprojective* if, at each vertex, the signed sum of the two-arrow return maps is zero.

<a id="pm-s3"></a>
## 3. Proof roadmap

- **s-coefficient** [claim](#stmt-lem-coefficient) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-coefficient): coinvariants and highest-generator presentation -> highest-to-highest coefficient criterion
- **s-flag-composition** [claim](#stmt-lem-flag-composition) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-flag-composition): bridging waypoints by flow orbits and an exact composition identity -> nonzero composition on the Cartan summand
- **s-invariant-moment** [claim](#stmt-lem-invariant-moment) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-invariant-moment): norm maximization on flag vectors -> real moment solution
- **s-schedule-moment** [claim](#stmt-lem-schedule-moment) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-schedule-moment): perturb, fold, compose extremal intertwiners, take limit -> real moment solution
- **s-strict-approx** [claim](#stmt-real-strict-approximation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-real-strict-approximation): semistable flags and convex combination with an auxiliary strict triple -> open set of strict regular labels
- **s-triangles** [claim](#stmt-real-triangles) / [proof](#proof-stmt-real-triangles): composition of pushes, inward estimate, fixed-point theorem -> triangles of every scale
- **s-decreasing-paths** [claim](#stmt-real-decreasing-paths) / [proof](#proof-stmt-real-decreasing-paths): rescale triangles and take limits -> decreasing dominant path
- **s-variation** [claim](#stmt-real-variation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-real-variation): first and second variation of the area functional -> independent velocity differences
- **s-exposure** [claim](#stmt-real-exposure) / [proof](#proof-stmt-real-exposure): insertion of intermediate states with compensating time shifts -> strict exposing functional
- **s-polytope** [claim](#stmt-real-polytope) / [proof](#proof-stmt-real-polytope): weight polytope of a torus orbit and Minkowski sums -> edges are root reflections
- **s-covers** [claim](#stmt-real-covers) / [proof](#proof-stmt-real-covers): inversion count against strict exposure -> jumps are cover reflections
- **s-basic-schedule** [claim](#stmt-prop-basic-schedule) / [proof](#proof-stmt-real-covers-2): area maximum for generic data then compactness limit -> basis schedule
- **s-blocker** [claim](#stmt-real-blocker) / [proof](#proof-stmt-real-blocker): strict inequalities persist in approximations -> no third-axis obstruction
- **s-block-reduction** [claim](#stmt-lem-block-reduction) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-block-reduction): restriction of moment solutions and superposition of block schedules -> combined integral schedules
- **s-hard-case** [claim](#stmt-lem-hard-case) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-hard-case): leaf removal and cycle determinant -> exceptional graph structure
- **s-closedness** [claim](#stmt-int-closedness) / [proof](#proof-stmt-int-closedness): bounded switch count and subsequence limit -> reduction to generic shapes
- **s-half-kernel** [claim](#stmt-int-half-kernel) / [proof](#proof-stmt-int-half-kernel): minors of signed graphs and telescoping reflections -> half relation with potential
- **s-continuation** [claim](#stmt-prop-continuation) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-continuation): continuation through collisions by rank-two sorting and supremum argument -> no local link
- **s-direct-links** [claim](#stmt-lem-direct-links) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-direct-links): insert bridge swaps and compute half-kernel derivative -> forbidden direct links
- **s-profile-rigidity** [claim](#stmt-knot-profile-rigidity) / [proof](#proof-stmt-knot-profile-rigidity): flag dimension count, direct sums fill an open set, Krull-Schmidt -> equal size and profile
- **s-strict-count** [claim](#stmt-prop-strict-count) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-strict-count): orbit dimension bound and Cauchy-Schwarz -> strict comparison excess
- **s-plateau** [claim](#stmt-lem-plateau) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-plateau): suffix reflection and graph cutting -> plateau phases
- **s-knot-matching** [claim](#stmt-knot-matching) / [proof](#proof-stmt-knot-matching): flow decomposition and shortcutting -> disjoint transfer units
- **s-knot-count** [claim](#stmt-prop-knot-count) / [proof](#proof-stmt-knot-matching-2): case analysis of single, pair-single and pair-pair comparisons -> monotone comparison function
- **s-integral-schedule** [claim](#stmt-thm-integral-schedule) / [proof](#proof-stmt-knot-matching-3): contradiction between strict count and monotonicity -> integral schedule
- **s-folding** [claim](#stmt-col-folding) / [proof](#proof-stmt-col-folding): perturb and fold into the dominant chamber with prefix induction -> integral downward reflection chain
- **s-common-walls** [claim](#stmt-col-common-walls) / [proof](#proof-stmt-col-common-walls): weight pairing computation -> allowed column coefficient
- **s-columns** [claim](#stmt-prop-columns) / [proof](#proof-stmt-col-common-walls-2): column-by-column intertwiners then flag composition -> nonzero intertwiner
- **s-preprojective** [claim](#stmt-prop-preprojective) / [proof](#proof-stmt-prop-preprojective): semicanonical generic coordinates with top and socle filtrations -> tensor criterion
- **s-threshold** [claim](#stmt-tail-threshold-bounds) / [proof](#proof-stmt-tail-threshold-bounds): row identity and descent along the chain -> threshold bounds
- **s-filtered-intersection** [claim](#stmt-lem-filtered-intersection) / [proof](#proof-stmt-lem-filtered-intersection): nested-support matching -> generic intersection dimension
- **s-centralizer** [claim](#stmt-tail-centralizer) / [proof](#proof-stmt-tail-centralizer): Jordan row module maps -> centralizer surjects on stabilizer
- **s-zero-tail** [claim](#stmt-prop-zero-tail) / [proof](#proof-stmt-tail-centralizer-2): doubling the chain with opposite colorings chosen generically -> doubled occurrence in smaller rank
- **s-main** [claim](#stmt-thm-main) / [proof](#proof-stmt-tail-centralizer-3): case split on zero coordinates, moments, integral schedule, columns; converse by powers of flag vectors -> saturation theorem

<a id="pm-s4"></a>
## 4. Engineering Triptych

### Panel 1 - epistemic contract

- **inv-scale-one-equivalence**: Under the root-lattice congruence for the three dominant integral weights of Spin(2n), a nonzero tensor invariant at any positive integral dilation exists exactly when one exists at the original weights.
- **inv-integral-stretching-essential**: The equivalence needs integral stretching: at a rational stretch the three scaled labels can have a sum outside the root lattice, so no invariant exists there even though the unscaled triple has one.

### Panel 2 - negative knowledge

- **anti-fractional-dilation**: Applying the saturation equivalence with a rational stretching factor instead of a positive integer. -> The scaled labels can leave the root lattice, so the triple has no invariant although the unscaled one does.

### Panel 3 - reference implementation and oracle

Applicability: `not_applicable`. The main saturation theorem and the integral-schedule theorem are existence and equivalence statements proved by compactness, area maximization, generic perturbation and limiting arguments over real parameters. The source gives no finite procedure with explicit steps that computes a schedule or an invariant, so there is nothing finite to execute against an oracle.

<a id="pm-s5"></a>
## 5. Domain engineering contracts

| domain | applies | strength | maturity | basis | why |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| software | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper proves existence statements about tensor invariants of Spin(2n). It specifies no executable procedure, interface or finite computation that a program could implement from it. |
| games_simulation | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The geodesic triangles and paths live in a symmetric space as part of a proof. They are not a dynamical system or simulation model with a stepping rule. |
| tools | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The result gives no decision procedure for tensor nonvanishing. The proof relies on area maximization, limits and compactness arguments that cannot be run as a checker. |
| algorithms | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Schedules are produced by existence arguments: generic maxima, subsequence extraction, and continuation by contradiction. No terminating procedure or complexity bound is stated, so no algorithm can be extracted. |
| data_structures | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The signed graphs, Bruhat intervals and Jordan rows are proof devices for a lattice argument, not a stored structure with operations and costs. |
| hardware_accelerators | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Nothing in the paper concerns arithmetic circuits, memory layout or parallel execution. The content is representation-theoretic existence. |
| crypto_security | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No hardness assumption, protocol or security reduction is given. The saturation statement says nothing about the difficulty of any problem. |
| machine_learning | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | The paper has no learning problem, loss or generalisation claim. Its optimisation of an area functional is a proof technique over a compact path class. |
| optimisation_operations_research | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Area maximization is used only to show a maximizer exists with independent velocity jumps. No optimisation problem is posed for solving, and no solver or bound is given. |
| distributed_systems | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | No communication, concurrency or fault model appears. Simultaneous switches are an ordering convention inside a single path. |
| other | False | none | not_applicable | inferred | Its natural use is as a theorem for representation theory and invariant theory, and it supplies no engineering construction. The integer stretching condition is essential and cannot be relaxed to rational scales. |

<a id="pm-s6"></a>
## 6. Applicability limits and search

- **limit-integral-stretching** (hypothesis): The equivalence holds only for positive integral stretching. Rational stretching fails: for the triple of weights all equal to twice the first unit vector, the scaled labels at one half have sum outside the root lattice, so no invariant exists there. Blocked use: ['Applying the saturation equivalence with a rational dilation factor', 'Inferring an invariant at a fractional scale from one at the original weights']
- **limit-root-lattice-congruence** (hypothesis): The theorem requires the sum of the three dominant integral weights to lie in the root lattice of type D. Without it the center acts nontrivially and no invariant can exist, so the equivalence says nothing outside this congruence class. Blocked use: ['Applying the theorem to weight triples whose sum is outside the root lattice', 'Using it as a general nonvanishing test without checking the congruence']
- **limit-type-d-rank** (hypothesis): The main theorem is stated only for the spin group of even rank parameter n at least two. Other types and the case n equal to one are not covered; for n equal to one the group is a torus and the claim reduces to the weight-sum equation. Blocked use: ['Transferring the unit saturation factor to symplectic, odd orthogonal or exceptional groups', 'Quoting the theorem for a group other than the even spin group']
- **limit-integral-schedule-zero-coordinate** (hypothesis): The integral-schedule theorem for type D needs the moving weight to have at most one zero coordinate and the lattice condition on the three labels. Longer zero tails are handled separately by a rank-reduction route using a doubled occurrence, not by this theorem. Blocked use: ['Applying the integral-schedule theorem directly to a moving weight with two or more zero coordinates', 'Dropping the lattice condition on the labels']
- **limit-nonconstructive-schedules** (nonconstructive): Schedules are obtained by area maximization over a compact path class, fixed-point arguments, subsequence limits and a proof by contradiction. No finite procedure and no complexity bound is given for computing a schedule or an invariant. Blocked use: ['Treating the argument as an algorithm with stated running time', 'Using it as a decision procedure for tensor nonvanishing']
- **limit-column-shape** (formal_scope): The passage from an integral schedule to a tensor intertwiner is proved for shapes concatenated from straight fundamental-column segments summing to the moving weight; the switch integrality is weaker than a saturated chain condition in a path model. Blocked use: ['Applying the column construction to arbitrary shapes', 'Reading integral switches as integral saturated chains']

<a id="pm-s7"></a>
## 7. Complete mathematical paper


We prove the saturation conjecture for $`\mathop{\mathrm{Spin}}\nolimits (2n)`$, <i>n</i> ≥ 2. If three dominant integral weights sum to an element of the root lattice, then the existence of a nonzero tensor invariant after a positive integral dilation implies the existence of one at the original weights.
